1	/-
2	  LowIR.Prog — the D7/D8 IR: LowIR.Ctrl's structured control flow plus
3	  activation-local CALLS and per-function stack FRAMES (LOWIR-DESIGN.md D7/D8).
4	
5	  D7 (calls): a program is an `Env : Name → FunDef`. A call runs the callee's
6	  body in a FRESH zero-initialized register file with only the parameters bound
7	  to the caller's argument values; on return, only the declared return registers
8	  are copied back into the caller's file. Call sites carry their own arities
9	  (arity-indexed `Vector`s, fixed-size by construction); `wf` cross-checks them
10	  against the env. NOT SSA — registers are mutable locals.
11	
12	  D8 (frames): `St` carries a semantic, program-unwritable stack pointer `sp`.
13	  Entering a function subtracts its `frameSize` (overflow below `stackLo` →
14	  `none`) and binds the new frame base to the function's designated `frameReg`
15	  (an ordinary IL register — the ONLY way a program observes sp). Returning
16	  restores the caller's `sp` structurally. No `alloca`.
17	
18	  Also new over Ctrl: `ld`/`sd` (64-bit little-endian load/store, byte order
19	  identical to `Rv64i.State.loadWord/storeWord`) and `annot` (Ext. 9 — a
20	  semantic no-op carrying a string).
21	
22	  `LowIR.Ctrl` is untouched (hex0 proofs stay green); `Outcome` is reused.
23	-/
24	import LowIR.Ctrl
25	
26	namespace LowIR.Prog
27	
28	open LowIR (Cond evalCond)
29	open LowIR.Ctrl (Outcome)
30	open Rv64i (Word Byte)
31	
32	abbrev Reg := Nat
33	abbrev Name := String
34	
35	/-- Structured statements. Ctrl's constructs minus its toy `call (g : Stmt)`,
36	    plus `ld`/`sd`/`annot` and the D7 named call. -/
37	inductive Stmt where
38	  | skip
39	  | seq    (a b : Stmt)
40	  | addi   (rd rs : Reg) (imm : BitVec 12)
41	  | add    (rd rs1 rs2 : Reg)
42	  | sub    (rd rs1 rs2 : Reg)
43	  | orr    (rd rs1 rs2 : Reg)
44	  | slli   (rd rs : Reg) (sh : Nat)
45	  | srli   (rd rs : Reg) (sh : Nat)
46	  | lbu    (rd rs : Reg) (imm : BitVec 12)
47	  | sb     (rbase rval : Reg) (imm : BitVec 12)
48	  | ld     (rd rs : Reg) (imm : BitVec 12)          -- rd := mem64[rs + sext imm]
49	  | sd     (rbase rval : Reg) (imm : BitVec 12)     -- mem64[rbase + sext imm] := rval
50	  | annot  (a : String)                             -- semantic no-op (Ext. 9)
51	  | ife    (c : Cond) (a b : Reg) (t e : Stmt)
52	  | while  (c : Cond) (a b : Reg) (body : Stmt)     -- continue scope
53	  | block  (body : Stmt)                            -- break scope
54	  | brkB   (k : Nat)
55	  | contL  (k : Nat)
56	  | ret
57	  | call   (argc rvc : Nat) (f : Name)
58	           (args : Vector Reg argc) (rets : Vector Reg rvc)
59	  | cref   (rd : Reg) (d : Name)     -- rd := address of const data object d
60	  | clen   (rd : Reg) (d : Name)     -- rd := length of const data object d
61	deriving Repr
62	
63	/-- A function: declared parameter/return registers (callee-side names), a
64	    frame size in bytes, the register that receives the frame base, a body. -/
65	structure FunDef where
66	  argc      : Nat
67	  rvc       : Nat
68	  params    : Vector Reg argc
69	  rets      : Vector Reg rvc
70	  frameSize : Nat
71	  frameReg  : Reg
72	  body      : Stmt
73	
74	abbrev Env := List (Name × FunDef)
75	
76	/-- The program's read-only data segment: named constant byte objects,
77	    referenced from code as slices via `cref` (address) + `clen` (length). -/
78	abbrev Data := List (Name × List Byte)
79	
80	/-- A whole program: functions plus const data. Where the data objects LIVE
81	    is not part of the program — the semantics takes a base map (`dbase`),
82	    ∀-quantifiable like D8's `sp₀`, so programs stay address-independent. -/
83	structure Program where
84	  env  : Env
85	  data : Data
86	
87	/-- Data-free view (keeps pre-data call sites unchanged). -/
88	instance : Coe Env Program := ⟨fun env => ⟨env, []⟩⟩
89	
90	/-- IL machine state: register file (x0 hardwired 0), byte memory, and the
91	    semantic stack pointer — no instruction writes `sp`; only call/return move i

def asBytes (l : List Nat) : List Byte := l.map (BitVec.ofNat 8)

/-- Run a hex-decoder from the library env; result (status, out bytes, len). -/
def hexRun (f : Name) (inp : List Nat) (cap fuel : Nat) : Nat × List Nat × Nat :=
  match run libProgram 0 fuel f
          [inBase, BitVec.ofNat 64 inp.length, outBase, BitVec.ofNat 64 cap]
          (memIn (asBytes inp)) SP0 with
  | some s => ((s.rget 14).toNat,
               (List.range (s.rget 6).toNat).map
                 (fun i => (s.mem (outBase + BitVec.ofNat 64 i)).toNat),
               (s.rget 6).toNat)
  | none   => (255, [], 0)

/-- hex0-as-a-function matches `Hex0.coreSpec` on the hex0 battery. -/
theorem hex0F_matches_spec :
theorem strtoullF_matches_spec :
    LowIR.Ctrl.Strtoull2.battery.all (fun inp =>
      (match run libProgram 0 100000 "strtoull" [inBase] (memIn (asBytes inp)) SP0 with
       | some s => (s.rget 12, (s.rget 14).toNat)
       | none   => (0xDEAD, 0))
      == LowIR.Ctrl.Strtoull2.strtoullConfSpec (asBytes inp)) = true := by
  native_decide

/-- strlen over a few strings. -/
theorem strlenF_ok :
    [("", 0), ("x", 1), ("Hello", 5), ("Hi!", 3)].all (fun tc =>
      (match run libProgram 0 1000 "strlen" [inBase]
               (memIn (asBytes (sbytes tc.1 ++ [0]))) SP0 with
       | some s => (s.rget 12).toNat
       | none   => 999)
      == tc.2) = true := by
  native_decide

/-- The driver end-to-end at the IL level: every observable as expected.
    checksum = the "Hello" buffer word + the hex1 output word
    ([00 FB FF FF FF] LE, zero-padded). -/
theorem main_il_ok :
    (run libProgram 0 100000 "main" [] (fun _ => 0) SP0).map
      (fun s => mainF.rets.toList.map s.rget)
    = some [3, 123456789, 0, 0, 5, 0, 5,
            (0x0000006F6C6C6548 : Word) + 0x000000FFFFFFFB00] := by
  native_decide

/-- The const-slice driver produces the SAME observables — inputs read from

3	
4	  Strategy: MEMORY-LOCALS (-O0). Every IL register gets an 8-byte slot in the
5	  function's machine frame; no register allocation. Correctness-first — the
6	  point is a differential-testing target for the D7/D8 semantics, and later the
7	  subject of `compile_sim`-style theorems (performance is a non-goal, N9).
8	
9	  Machine frame (grows down; all offsets sp-relative, sp = x2 after prologue):
10	
11	        sp + 0                  : saved ra
12	        sp + 8*(1+r)            : slot for IL register r   (r = 0..maxReg)
13	        sp + 8*(maxReg+2)       : user frame (frameSize bytes) — the address
14	                                  handed to the IL `frameReg`
15	        total = 8*(maxReg+2) + frameSize   (must fit imm12: ≤ 2000, checked)
16	
17	  Physical registers: x2=sp, x1=ra, x5/x6 = t0/t1 scratch, x10..x17 = a0..a7
18	  argument/return marshalling (so argc,rvc ≤ 8, checked). Nothing is live
19	  across an IL statement except the slots, so calls need no caller-save logic.
20	
21	  IL zero-init is matched EXACTLY: the prologue `sd x0` into every non-param,
22	  non-frameReg slot (differential tests stay honest; definite-assignment
23	  optimization comes later).
24	
25	  Pipeline: lower to `SymInstr` (labels symbolic, calls by name) → lay out the
26	  whole program (all instructions are 4 bytes) → resolve branches/jumps/calls
27	  to relative offsets (range-checked) → `Rv64i.Instr` list. Encoding to bytes
28	  reuses `LowIR.encode`/`asmBytes`. Entry convention: the program starts with
29	  a single `jal ra, <entry>` stub at `codeBase`; when the entry function
30	  returns, pc = codeBase+4 — run the machine with `runFuel (codeBase+4)`.
31	-/
32	import LowIR.Prog
33	
34	namespace LowIR.Compile
35	
36	open LowIR (Cond condInstr jal0)
37	open LowIR.Prog (Reg Name FunDef Env Data Program wfProgram pad8 dataOffsetsFrom dataSegment)
38	open Rv64i (Word Byte Instr)

41	local notation "PStmt" => LowIR.Prog.Stmt
42	
43	/-! ### Physical register roles -/
44	
45	def SP : Reg := 2
46	def RA : Reg := 1
47	def T0 : Reg := 5
48	def T1 : Reg := 6
49	/-- Argument/return register `a_i` = x10+i. -/

54	/-- Position-independent instruction stream: concrete instructions, label
55	    markers (emit nothing), and label/name-relative control transfers. -/
56	inductive SymInstr where
57	  | ins   (i : Instr)                          -- concrete, position-independent
58	  | label (l : Nat)                            -- marker (0 bytes)
59	  | br    (c : Cond) (a b : Reg) (l : Nat)     -- branch (phys regs) to label
60	  | jmp   (l : Nat)                            -- jal x0, label
61	  | callf (f : Name)                           -- jal ra, function
62	  | cref  (d : Name)                           -- T0 := address of data object
63	deriving Repr
64	
65	/-! ### Per-function lowering -/
66	
67	/-- Slot offset of IL register `r` (see frame picture above). -/
68	def slotOff (r : Reg) : Nat := 8 * (1 + r)
69	
70	/-- Load IL register `r`'s slot into physical register `t`
71	    (IL x0 is hardwired zero — never read its slot). -/
72	def loadSlot (r : Reg) (t : Reg) : List SymInstr :=
73	  if r = 0 then [.ins (.addi t 0 0)]

75

77	    (IL writes to x0 are discarded — emit nothing). -/
78	def storeSlot (r : Reg) (t : Reg) : List SymInstr :=
79	  if r = 0 then []
80	  else [.ins (.sd SP t (BitVec.ofNat 12 (slotOff r)))]
81	
82	/-- Fresh-label supply. -/

84	def fresh : M Nat := modifyGet fun n => (n, n + 1)

18	namespace LowIR.CompileTests
19	
20	open LowIR.Prog (Env FunDef Name Program)
21	open LowIR.Compile
22	open Rv64i (Word Byte)
23	
24	def CODE  : Word := 0x10000
25	def DATA  : Word := 0x20000
26	def SPTOP : Word := 0x80000
27	def HALT  : Word := CODE + 4
28	
29	/-- Initial machine: the full blob (code + const-data segment) at CODE,
30	    `args` in a0.., sp = SPTOP, `data` at DATA (everything else reads 0). -/
31	def machineOf (blob : List Byte) (args : List Word) (data : List Byte) :
32	    Rv64i.State :=
33	  { reg := fun i =>
34	      if i = 2 then SPTOP
35	      else if 10 ≤ i ∧ i < 10 + args.length then args.getD (i - 10) 0
36	      else 0
37	    pc  := CODE
38	    mem := LowIR.loadMem CODE blob DATA data }
39	
40	/-- IL side: final callee state of `Prog.run`. -/
41	def ilFinal (P : Program) (entry : Name) (args : List Word) (data : List Byte)
42	    (fuel : Nat) : Option LowIR.Prog.St :=
43	  LowIR.Prog.run P 0 fuel entry args (LowIR.memOf DATA data) SPTOP
44	
45	/-- Machine side: compile, load, run to HALT (`none` if compile fails or the
46	    machine doesn't reach the halt pc in `fuel`). -/
47	def mcFinal (P : Program) (entry : Name) (args : List Word) (data : List Byte)
48	    (fuel : Nat) : Option Rv64i.State := do
49	  let blob ← progBytes P entry
50	  let m := Rv64i.runFuel HALT fuel (machineOf blob args data)
51	  if m.pc = HALT then some m else none

333	def Layout.inCode (L : Layout) (a : Word) : Prop := L.codeBase ≤ a ∧ a < L.endPc

## Phase 0.1 complete — the P1 frame-padding oracle (commit `cedb25c`)

**What I implemented** (`lean/LowIR/Prog.lean`):
- Added the `pad : Name → Nat` oracle to `frameEnter`/`exec`/`run` exactly per RESUME-PROGSIM §2. `frameReg` (the only IL-observable frame base) stays at `spCaller − frameSize` — position unchanged; the propagated `sp` drops an extra `pad` bytes. The overflow check now reads `sp ≥ stackLo + frameSize + pad` (stack budget subsumed, as the plan promised).
- The compiler/shim/QEMU are untouched (frozen). `pad = fun _ => 0` reproduces the old semantics — **the entire differential suite + all `Prog.lean` `#guard`s re-green unchanged** (verified via full `lake build LowIRCompile`).

**Executable validation of P1** (`CompileTests.lean` Stage 4c) — this is the part I most wanted to nail down before any lemma leans on the idea:
- `framesAgree` checks every stack byte the IL wrote matches the machine byte-for-byte.
- `p1_chain_userPad` / `p1_rec_userPad` **pass** at `pad = userPad` (3-deep chain, 5-deep recursion).
- `p1_chain_pad0_diverges` / `p1_rec_pad0_diverges` **prove the old `pad = 0` IL diverges** from the machine the moment a depth-≥1 callee writes its frame.

I hand-probed the addresses first to be sure it wasn't vacuous: at `pad=userOff` the parked values land at `SPTOP-8 / SPTOP-136 / SPTOP-264` on *both* sides; at `pad=0` the IL writes into what the machine uses as spill slots (`(0,40,2)` at `SPTOP-16`). So P1 closes a genuine, observable address gap.

- Added `pad : Name → Nat` to `frameEnter`/`exec`/`run`. `frameReg` stays at `spCaller−frameSize`; `sp` drops an extra `pad`, so at `pad := userOff` IL `sp` ≡ machine `x2` at every depth. Compiler/shim/QEMU untouched (frozen); `pad = fun _ => 0` re-greens the entire differential suite + all `#guard`s.
- **Executably validated it isn't vacuous**: `framesAgree` + `p1_chain_*`/`p1_rec_*` — IL frame memory matches the machine byte-for-byte at `pad=userPad`, and *provably diverges* at the old `pad=0` once a depth-≥1 callee writes its frame. I hand-probed the addresses first (`(0,40,2)` at the slot boundary) to be sure.

**`2fa8faf` — `ProgSim/Defs.lean` (A)**: `Layout`/`layoutOf` from the frozen `compileProgT`, `Installed` (+ computable `codeInstalledB`/`dataInstalledB`, `#guard`'d by loading real blobs into the trusted `Rv64i` machine), and `execT` — the write-footprint instrumentation — with `runT` + `execT_erase` (sorry). Footprints `#guard`'d exactly: sub3→0 bytes, frameLocal's `sd`→its 8 frame addresses, caller inherits them. New `LowIRProgSim` lib target added up-front (build-root trap handled).

**`cdcbf0f` — `ProgSim/Defs.lean` (B)**: the §3.1 relation — `MachPriv` (+ `machPrivB`, `#guard`'d: blob/slot private, user-frame *not*, plus the P1 tiling identity `sp+userOff = sp0−frameSize`), `MachStack`, `StInv` (plain equality thanks to P1), `SimPre`, `userPad` — and the fully-stated self-contained payoff `prog_sim` (sorry).

**One deliberate scoping call**: I did *not* state `lower_sim`/`call_sim` (§3.2). They require the compile-time `Emitted` predicate that Phase 2 characterizes and the vertical slice validates against the oracle — stating them blind is exactly the "wrong statement costs weeks" failure mode the plan flags. They land with the slice, where they can be checked. `prog_sim` needs none of that and is the composable corollary.

Every relation definition has an executable `#guard` shadow that passes on real compiled programs, per the §6 discipline. Frontier recorded in memory (`progsim-campaign-frontier`).

**Next** (cold-start, per §8.3): prove `execT_erase` (mechanical), port the one-layer `exec_*` unfolder lemmas (Phase 0.2), then the **vertical slice** — state+prove `lower_sim`'s straight-line cases and check a sub3-class end-to-end corollary. That's the go/no-go checkpoint for the relation design, and a substantial proof push best taken as its own run.

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  12:06:47; model 12.3s

`execT_erase` is proved and committed (`49201ec`).

## What landed

Replaced the `sorry` with a real proof, structured around a clean helper:

- **`execT_map_exec`** — the core fact: erasing the write footprint via `Option.map eraseW` turns `execT` into `exec` *exactly*. Since both functions recurse only at `fuel` (structurally), a single fuel induction with the IH quantified over all `stmt`/`s` discharges every case:
  - value ops (skip/addi/sd/…) → `rfl`
  - `ife` → `apply_ite` pushes the map through the branch, then the IH
  - `seq`/`block`/`while` → `cases` on the sub-`execT` result; the footprints differ by `++` but `eraseW` drops them, so the outcomes match
  - `call` → `cases h : …` on lookup/arity/frameEnter (the `cases`+`simp [h]` pattern, since `split` only rewrites one of the twin LHS/RHS matches), then the body via the IH
- **`execT_erase`** falls out as a one-liner: `rw [← execT_map_exec, h]`.

**Verification**: build green, and `#print axioms execT_erase` → `[propext, Quot.sound]` only — no `sorryAx`, cleaner even than the plan's target (no `Classical.choice`).

A couple of Lean gotchas worth noting from the process: `while` is a reserved keyword so the case alternative needed `«while»`, and `cases <scrutinee>` chokes when the match-arm binds a variable the other side reuses (`some fd`) — `cases h : … ; simp [h]` is the reliable pattern there.

The only remaining `sorry` in `Defs.lean` is now `prog_sim` (the payoff, deferred with `lower_sim`/`call_sim` to the vertical-slice phase). Next up per the plan: port the one-layer `exec_*` unfolder lemmas (Phase 0.2), then the vertical slice.

2	  strtoull (base-10 core) correctness — and the proof-ergonomics finding for the new

5	  The headline: the reasoning primitives for `break`/`block`/`ret` are *trivial*. Each

7	  `normal`, a `while` passing `brk` through, a `seq` short-circuiting on `brk`. So the
8	  outcome machinery costs almost nothing to reason about — it just adds a few uniform
9	  equations alongside the `normal` ones we already used for strlen.
10

12	  induction on the number of leading digits — the same shape as `strlen`'s loop lemma,
13	  with two additions: the accumulator step `acc*10 = (acc<<3)+(acc<<1)` (a BitVec
14	  identity), and the terminating iteration returning `brk 0` (caught by the `block`).
15	  Stated with the invariant here; the induction is deferred (`sorry`), backed by the

31	    exec (f+1) (.add rd r1 r2) s = some (s.rset rd (s.rget r1 + s.rget r2), .normal) := by simp [exec]

34	    exec (f+1) (.orr rd r1 r2) s = some (s.rset rd (s.rget r1 ||| s.rget r2), .normal) := by simp [exec]

/-- Load the little-endian 64-bit word at address `a`. -/
def State.loadWord (s : State) (a : Word) : Word :=

/-- Store the 64-bit word `v` at address `a`, little-endian (8 byte stores,
    low byte first -- compositional for the proof side). -/
def State.storeWord (s : State) (a : Word) (v : Word) : State :=
  (((((((s.storeByte a (v.setWidth 8)
    ).storeByte (a + 1) ((v >>> 8).setWidth 8)
    ).storeByte (a + 2) ((v >>> 16).setWidth 8)
    ).storeByte (a + 3) ((v >>> 24).setWidth 8)
    ).storeByte (a + 4) ((v >>> 32).setWidth 8)
    ).storeByte (a + 5) ((v >>> 40).setWidth 8)
    ).storeByte (a + 6) ((v >>> 48).setWidth 8)
    ).storeByte (a + 7) ((v >>> 56).setWidth 8)

/-- Execute one instruction. Undecodable instructions leave the state stuck
    (pc unchanged), which the proof side treats as a non-final, non-advancing
    state. -/
def step (s : State) : State :=
  let next := s.pc + 4

    (s.storeWord a v).loadWord a = v := by
  simp only [State.storeWord, State.loadWord, State.storeByte]
  simp only [BitVec.add_right_inj, BitVec.reduceEq, if_true, if_false, ite_true, ite_false,
             BitVec.reduceAdd]
  apply BitVec.eq_of_getLsbD_eq; intro i

             BitVec.getLsbD_ushiftRight]
  intro hi

  rcases (show i < 8 ∨ 8 ≤ i ∧ i < 16 ∨ 16 ≤ i ∧ i < 24 ∨ 24 ≤ i ∧ i < 32 ∨
             32 ≤ i ∧ i < 40 ∨ 40 ≤ i ∧ i < 48 ∨ 48 ≤ i ∧ i < 56 ∨ 56 ≤ i from by omega)
    with h | h | h | h | h | h | h | h <;>
    (cases hb : v.getLsbD i <;> simp_all <;> omega)

/-- A `storeWord` at `a` leaves `mem x` untouched for any `x` outside the eight
    stored addresses (stated with the exact literal forms so `simp` fires). -/
theorem storeWord_mem_of_ne (s : State) (a v x : Word)
    (h0 : x ≠ a) (h1 : x ≠ a+1) (h2 : x ≠ a+2) (h3 : x ≠ a+3)

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  16:30:21; model 14.5s

> 💭 (thinking)

Done. The round-trip proof is now genuinely fast.

## Answer to your question

No — `cases hb` was **not** a 256-way blowup. The 3 s came from a different place:

- The structure was 8-way `rcases` (one per byte) × `cases hb` = **16 leaves**, and each leaf was closed by `simp_all`. `simp_all` re-simplifies the *entire hypothesis context* from scratch every time, ~0.19 s × 16 ≈ 3 s. It's linear in the leaves, but each leaf is expensive — a heavy tactic in the hot path, not a combinatorial explosion.

## The fix

Factor the per-byte reasoning into **one reusable lemma** proven once, then compose:

- **`byte_bit`**: bit `i` of the shifted byte `c` equals `v`'s bit `i` iff `i ∈ [c, c+8)`. Proven once (two cases: in-window / below-window).
- **`loadWord_storeWord_same`**: after `simp only [byte_bit]`, the goal is just the OR of eight windows tiling `[0,64)` — a handful of `omega`s. No `simp_all` anywhere.

Two toolchain gotchas the speed turned on, both now documented in the file's proof-note:
1. **`omega` rejects a Bool-valued goal** → convert with `rw [Bool.eq_iff_iff]` first.
2. That `rw` leaves an **`↔ True` wrapper** `omega` can't strip → put `iff_true` in the `simp` set. (This was the last stubborn `i ≤ 7` "counterexample" — the disjunction was correct, but `omega` was staring at `disjunction ↔ True` instead of `disjunction`.)

**Result:** 12 s → 3 s → **~0.6 s wall (≈985 ms to build the whole file, proof elaboration is milliseconds)**. Axioms unchanged (`[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]` — `Classical.choice` is `omega`'s, as before).

Committed as `f513c27`, `lake build LowIRProgSim` green (the only `sorry` is the Phase-4 `prog_sim` target). Next up per the campaign plan: the rest of Phase 3 (StInv slot algebra) → the `lower_sim`/`call_sim` vertical slice.

9	## 0. Mission and payoff
10	
11	Prove: **if the D7/D8 IL says a program computes something, the compiled RV64I
12	bytes compute the same thing on the trusted `Rv64i` model.** This is the T1
13	of `LowIR.lean:312` re-targeted at the real IR (functions, frames, calls,
14	const data) — the pass that amortizes the flat-PC simulation cost paid
15	per-program in `Hex0/Refine.lean` (~3400 lines for ONE program) into ONE
16	proof reused by every program. After it, a structured-altitude proof like

18	functions get machine-level correctness for the price of their (easy)
19	IL-level proofs.

46	## 2. THE design obstacle, and decision P1 (resolve before any proving)
47	
48	**Address divergence.** IL frames and machine frames live at different
49	addresses: the machine frame adds `[ra][slot per IL reg]` overhead, so after
50	one call the IL's `frameReg` value (= IL `sp`) and the machine's user-frame
51	base (= machine `sp + userOff`) differ, and diverge cumulatively with call
52	depth. Any value derived from `frameReg` (hex1's whole label table!) differs
53	between altitudes. A plain-equality simulation relation is therefore FALSE
54	for any program that touches its frame, and a CompCert-style memory
55	injection (per-value address translation) is unsound on our flat `BitVec 64`
56	words without provenance — you cannot tell a pointer from an integer. That
57	is the borrow layer's future job, not this cut's.
58	
59	**Decision P1 — the frame-padding oracle: make the addresses EQUAL by
60	∀-quantified padding, with the compiler UNCHANGED.**
61	
62	The key arithmetic fact (verify in the code: `Compile.lean` prologue,
63	`Prog.frameEnter`): the compiler already places the user frame at the TOP of
